Every topic in one line

WkTopicOne-line answer
W5YARN and its three componentsYARN is Hadoop 2's resource manager: one ResourceManager gives out containers, a NodeManager runs them on each node, and one ApplicationMaster manages each job.
W5YARN fault toleranceWhen a NodeManager stops heartbeating, the RM marks its containers failed and the AM requests replacements on other nodes.
W5Schedulers and capacity planningThe Capacity Scheduler splits the cluster into queues with guaranteed shares and lets queues borrow idle capacity.
W5Row vs columnar storage and ParquetColumnar storage keeps each column's values together on disk, so a query reads only the columns it needs.
W5File formats: Avro, Parquet, ORCAvro is a row format built for schema evolution; Parquet and ORC are columnar formats built for analytics.
W5Compression codecs and splittabilityA splittable file can be read from any point, so multiple Mappers process different parts at the same time.
W6Why Spark: the MapReduce iteration bottleneckMapReduce writes every intermediate result to HDFS, so iterative algorithms pay disk I/O on every pass; Spark caches the data in RAM once.
W6Spark architectureSpark has three tiers: a Driver that builds the DAG and schedules tasks, a Cluster Manager that allocates resources, and Executors that run tasks and cache data.
W6RDDs and lineage-based fault toleranceAn RDD is a read-only, partitioned collection of records that Spark rebuilds from its lineage if a partition is lost.
W6Transformations, actions and lazy evaluationLazy evaluation means Spark records transformations in a DAG and runs nothing until an action is called.
W6Narrow vs wide dependencies, stages and shuffleA wide dependency is one where an output partition needs data from many input partitions, which forces a shuffle and a new stage.
W6DataFrames, Spark SQL and the ecosystemA DataFrame is a distributed table with named columns and a schema, optimised by Catalyst.
W7Hive architecture and query compilationHive is a data warehouse layer on Hadoop that compiles SQL-like HiveQL queries into MapReduce, Tez or Spark jobs over files on HDFS.
W7Schema-on-read and HiveQL vs SQLSchema-on-read means Hive applies the table's column types when data is queried, not when it is loaded.
W7Partitioning and partition pruningPartitioning stores a Hive table in separate HDFS sub-directories by the value of a column, so queries filtering on it read only matching directories.
W7Bucketing and bucket map joinBucketing splits each Hive partition into a fixed number of files by hash(column) % N.
W7Presto and the SQL-on-Hadoop enginesPresto is Meta's in-memory SQL engine that answers interactive queries over HDFS in seconds by never writing intermediate results to disk.
W7Case study: Spotify Discover WeeklyDiscover Weekly is Spotify's weekly recommendation playlist, built from playlist co-occurrence in HiveQL and ALS collaborative filtering in Spark, scheduled by Luigi.
W8From point-to-point ETL to event pipelinesPoint-to-point ETL connects every pair of systems with its own job, giving O(N²) jobs.
W8Batch ingestion: Sqoop and FlumeSqoop is a batch tool that copies tables in bulk between an RDBMS and HDFS.
W8Kafka: the append-only logA Kafka offset is the integer position of a message inside a partition.
W8Why Kafka is fastZero-copy uses sendfile() to send data from disk to the network without copying it through user space.
W8Lambda and Kappa architecturesKappa architecture drops Lambda's batch layer and reprocesses history by replaying the Kafka log.
W8Data lakes and the data swampA data swamp is a data lake with no governance: files without metadata, owners or quality checks.
W9Tufte: data-ink, chartjunk, lie factorThe data-ink ratio is the share of a chart's ink that encodes data, and Tufte says to maximise it.
W9Shneiderman's mantra and choosing the chartShneiderman's mantra is "overview first, zoom and filter, then details on demand".
W9Overplotting: alpha, hexbin, KDEOverplotting is when millions of scatter points overlap into a solid blob that shows nothing.
W9Latency: data cubes and AQPA data cube is a set of pre-computed aggregate tables over combinations of dimensions, read instead of the raw fact table.
W9Streaming dashboards and toolsGrafana is the tool for real-time ops dashboards that poll a time-series database every few seconds.
W9Case study: NYT COVID-19 trackerThe NYT COVID tracker showed that cleaning data from 50 sources was harder than drawing the charts.
W10Social media as a graph: communities, influence, Twitter's shapeCommunity detection finds groups of nodes more densely linked to each other than to the rest of the graph, usually with the Louvain algorithm.
W10Sentiment (VADER), trend detection and ethicsVADER is a lexicon-and-rule-based sentiment analyser for social media that outputs a compound score in [−1, +1].
W10Time series, ARIMA and Prophet at scaleProphet is a time series forecasting model that splits a series into trend, seasonality, holidays and noise: y(t) = g(t) + s(t) + h(t) + ε(t).
W10Anomaly detection: STL and Isolation ForestAn anomaly is a data point that does not conform to the expected pattern of the series.
W10Healthcare big data, privacy and AlphaFoldFederated learning trains a model at each hospital and shares only model updates, so raw patient records never leave the site.
W10Syndromic surveillance and the Google Flu Trends lessonSyndromic surveillance uses non-traditional signals, such as posts, pharmacy sales and ER notes, that show illness before a diagnosis is reported.
